2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 歐洲央行上周四(9/10)為年內第二次上調利率，以應對能源價格上漲帶來的通膨
壓力 ， 並警告物價壓力可能比先前預期更久 ， 推動市場加大對後續進一步收緊政
策的押注 。 歐洲央行總裁拉加德形容此次决定爲”無需多想的决定”， 並警告通膨
回落至 2%目標的時間點目前預計在 2027 年底。歐洲央行預期明年平均通膨爲
2.5%，高於 6 月的 2.3%；2027 年核心通膨預期爲 2.6%，高於此前的 2.5%。
⚫ 軍事消息人士稱，與伊朗結盟的胡塞武裝上周四(9/10)奪取葉門港口城市的控制
權 ， 並沿紅海海岸向南推進至戰略島嶼地區 。 美國總統川普表示 ， 他預計伊朗戰
爭將在美國中期選舉後結束。若胡塞武裝控制這條與 荷姆茲海峽同樣重要的水
道 ， 其支持者伊朗將在與美國的戰爭中獲得關鍵戰略優勢 ， 並進一步推高油價 。
⚫ 美國勞工部勞工統計局上週四(9/10)公布 8 月生産者物價指數 PPI 年增 5.4%，

高於市場預期的 5.3％。週五(9/11)公布 8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 3.4%與前
月相同，核心 CPI 年增率 2.4%符合市場預期，月增率達 0.3%高於市場預期。
FED WATCH顯示數據公布後推升市場對於聯準會本周將升息的預期。
⚫ 日本央行數據顯示，8 月國內企業物價指數較去年同期上漲 7.6%。增幅高於市
場預估中值 7.4%。7 月漲幅經修正後爲 7.7%；8 月進口物價指數同比上漲 24.8%，
7 月修正後爲上漲 29.3%， 表明當月日圓貶值以及中東局勢影響推高進口成本和
整體通膨，同時鞏固市場對日本央行在 9 月會議進一步升息預期。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
美元兌新台幣匯率走勢
Sep/14/2026
＜上週走勢＞
上週台幣匯價高點為 31.455，低點在 31.698，以 31.638 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 0.8 分。
上週台幣先升後貶，週初，受到市場對於日本央行 9 月升息的預期升溫，日圓
走升帶動美元指數跌破 99 整數價位低檔震盪 ， 加以台股高檔震盪 ， 外資延續匯入的
走勢，帶動台幣升破31.500 至 31.455 週內高點；臨近週末，美國公布8 月生產者物
價指數年增率由上個月的 4.8%上升至 5.4%，數據公布後，FEDWATCH顯示聯準會
9 月升息機率攀升至 7 成，美元指數回到 99 上方，台股高點回落，外資偏向匯出，
台幣由升轉貶至 31.698 週內低點，終場在央行調節之下收在 31.638。
＜未來分析＞
預估本週台幣區間震盪。國際市場方面，上週五公布美國 8 月核心消費者物價
指數月增率 0.3%高於市場預期的 0.2%，數據公布後，提高本週聯準會升息的機率

至接近 9 成，此外中東局勢仍有升溫風險，油價持續高檔震盪，美元指數在 99 整數
關口盤整；就台灣市場來看，台灣 8 月出口衝上 824 億美元，創下歷年單月新高紀
錄，月增 9.4%，年增 41%，展現極為強勁的成長動能。顯示受惠於 AI 需求全面爆
發，帶動外銷出口暢旺，基本面強勁持續支撐台幣的表現；盤勢上，匯價逢低吸引
進口商及政府基金買盤進場，逢高則面臨出口商賣美金，且本週台美均有利率決策
會議，預估匯率波動將加劇。綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間震盪。
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.400-31.900

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/14/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週人民幣震盪走升。週初，雖然美伊戰火未平，帶動油價及美債利率高漲，
但市場對於日本央行 9 月升息預期升溫，日圓偏升帶動美元指數跌破 99 價位後低
檔震盪，人民幣延續偏升走勢，走升至6.7022 週內高點；臨近週末，美國公布8 月
生產者物價指數 5.4%優於市場預期 5.3%， 加以8 月核心消費者物價指數月增率 0.3%
優於市場預期的 0.2%，數據公布後，FEDWATCH顯示本週聯準會 9 月升息預期大
幅攀升至近 9 成，美元指數回到99 上方，人民幣回吐部分週初以來的漲幅，終場境
外人民幣收在 6.7071，週線升值 8 點，境內人民幣則收在6.7079，週線升值 29 點。  
＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣區間偏升。國際市場方面，美國通膨數據表現優於市場預期，
加以油價攀升至每桶 100 美元上方 ， 美債殖利率位居高檔 ， 美元指數也在99 上方盤
整，關注本週聯準會最新利率決議，預計在會議結果出爐以前，聯準會升息預期將
支撐美元指數，限制人民幣升值的空間。中國國內方面，人行中間價格緩步偏升，
上週升破 6.7800 價位，持續釋放人民幣偏強信號，支撐人民幣延續升值的方向。技
術面方面 ，USD/CNH 匯價居於各天期均線下方 ，且均線向下發散 ，加以匯價由低點
向上反彈測試 6.7150 關鍵價位後面臨壓力，顯示空方動能較盛，預計人民幣將延續
升值的態勢。綜合以上，預估本週人民幣走勢區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7300

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/14/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY 震盪下跌 。週初 ，因市場預期日本央行將加快升息步伐及資金將
回流日本，使 USD/JPY 跌破至 155 整數關卡之下；週間，USD/JPY 延續跌勢最低
來到 152.87 後反彈，後續大致在 153 至 154 間波動；臨近週末，美國公布 8 月 PPI
及 CPI 數據顯示通膨仍處高位 ，USD/JPY 一度反彈至154.6 後回落 ， 終場收在153.54，
週線大跌 270 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、上週公布日本第二季 GDP
較上季增長 0.4%，季增年率也由 1.1%上升至 1.4%，主因為企業資本支出改善，而
7 月名目薪資年增 4.7%為 30 年來最大漲幅，而實質薪資年增 2.4%也為 5 年多來最
大漲幅，顯示日本經濟復甦力道較原先預期強勁，進一步強化市場對於日央升息的
預期，有利日圓表現。二、美國 8 月 CPI 及 PPI 數據顯示美國通膨仍處高位，強化
市場對聯準會升息預期，且中東局勢升溫，帶動油價突破 100 美元，預期將支撐美
元，限縮 USD/JPY 下行空間。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，USD/JPY 匯價多次向
下測試 153 整數關卡未能有效突破，顯示下方有買盤支撐，而 155 關卡附近則為前
波壓力位，因此短線先以區間看待。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的
可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：152.00-157.00 
前週交易區間 
152.87 156.27

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Sep/14/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 小幅下跌。 週初 ，EUR/USD 大致在 1.1600 至 1.1635 窄幅波動；
週間，市場等待歐洲央行利率決議及 美國通膨數據，EUR/USD 持續在 1.1615 至
1.1653 波動；週四晚間公布歐洲央行利率決議如預期升息 1 碼，而美國 8 月 PPI 高
於預期使市場對聯準會本月升息預期上升，EUR/USD 下行跌破 1.16 整數關口；臨
近週末，美國公布 8 月 CPI 顯示通膨仍具韌性，帶動 EUR/USD 走低來至 1.1568，
終場收在 1.1598，週線下跌 15 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、上週歐洲央行公布利率決
議如預期升息一碼，總裁拉加德表示即使面對能源價格衝擊，歐洲經濟第二季依然
展現韌性，預期第三季仍會延續，但未來通膨仍面臨上行風險，偏鷹派言論使市場
對於歐洲央行未來持續升息的預期上升，將帶給歐元支撐。二、美國 8 月 PPI 月增
0.4%符合市場預期 ， 年增5.4%高於預期 ，而 8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%年增 3.4%符合預期 ，
顯示通膨仍維持高位，使市場對聯準會升息預期增加，將支撐美元，不利歐元匯價
上行。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖一度突破 1.1630 年線但未
能站穩，顯示上方仍有賣壓，而下方1.1550 附近則有支撐，在有效突破前以區間震
盪看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1530-1.1680

                         新台幣利率走勢分析

115.09.07-115.09.11 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.69% 
公債－10 年期-A15106 1.92 % ~ 1.921% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.822% ~ 1.823%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ，本週央行連日減發定存單 ， 使市場資金呈現平穩態勢 ， 且
資金需求不若先前，銀行間交投較為清淡，令短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.69%，利率較上週上升 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週 5 年期指標券 A15108 成交在約 1.82%，10 年期指標券 A15106 成交在約
1.92%。 
美伊衝突升溫，帶動國際油價持續走揚，加劇市場對通膨之疑慮，加上美國8
月非農就業人數大增、8 月 PPI 年增率高於市場預期，市場預估聯準會下週升
息機率升至七成，帶動美債利率大幅反彈；台灣央行將於下週召開理監事會
議 ， 在台灣經濟成長與通膨維持高位下 ， 台灣央行面臨緊縮壓力 ， 預期台債殖
利率面臨走升壓力 ， 預估5 年券區間為 1.83%~1.90%，10 年券區間為 1.93%~2%。

2026 年 10 月 23 日 全6 頁

Indicators Update 
2026 年 9 月全国消費者物価 
サービスや非耐久財で価格上昇、コアCPI は9 カ月ぶり＋2％台

経済調査部 エコノミスト 横田 凱

[要約] 
◼ 2026 年 9 月の全国コアCPI（除く生鮮食品）は前年比＋2.1％と前月から伸び率が拡大
し、9 カ月ぶりに 2％台となった。生鮮食品やエネルギーを除いた全国新コアコア CPI
は同＋2.4％と、こちらも前月から伸び率が拡大し、6 カ月ぶりに 2％台に達した。食
料品、旅行関連、紙製品などの値上げと、政策要因の剥落が重なり、サービスや非耐
久消費財（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー）の伸び率が高まった。 
◼ コア CPI の前年比の動きを財・サービス別に見ると、サービス、非耐久消費財（除く
生鮮食品、エネルギー） 、耐久消費財、半耐久消費の伸び率が拡大し、エネルギーのマ
イナス幅が縮小した。 
◼ 先行きの物価上昇率について、コア CPI 上昇率は 26 年度末にかけて前年比＋3％超ま
で高まると見込んでいる。原油高が、エネルギーにとどまらず、幅広い財・サービス
の価格へ波及するとみられる。円安や、賃上げの価格転嫁と併せ、基調的な物価への
上昇圧力となるだろう。27 年度は、こうした物価上昇圧力は継続する一方で、飲食料
品にかかる消費税率の引き下げにより、コアCPI の上昇率は同＋1％台に縮小するとみ
られる。

### Page 2

1

2 / 6

26 年9 月 CPI：食料品の値上げや政策要因の剥落でコア CPI の伸び率は拡大 
2026 年9 月の全国コアCPI（除く生鮮食品）は前年比＋2.1％と前月から0.4％pt 伸び率が拡
大し、9 カ月ぶりに 2％台となった。また、全国新コアコア CPI（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー）
（同＋2.4％）も前月から0.5％pt 伸び率が拡大し、6 カ月ぶりに2％台に達した（図表1）。 
食料品、旅行関連、紙製品などの価格上昇と、政策要因の剥落が重なって、サービスや非耐
久消費財（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー）の伸び率が高まった。 
食料品については、コメ価格は下落基調にある一方で、他の品目で価格が上昇しており、生
鮮食品除く食料は前年比＋2.9％（8 月：同＋2.7％）と、14 カ月ぶりに伸び率が拡大した。旅
行関連は、大型連休が25 年9 月より長かったため需要が強く、価格を押し上げたとみられる。 
また、東京都の政策要因の剥落が全国の指数にも影響した。25 年 9 月に保育料無償化を拡充
したことの裏の影響や、水道料減免が終了したことで、コア指数の前年比が 0.1％pt 程度押し
上げられた。

3 / 6

食料品などの値上げや政策要因の剥落で、サービスや非耐久財が上昇 
コア CPI の前年比の動きを財・サービス別に見ると（図表 3）、サービス（前年比＋1.5％） 、
非耐久消費財（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー） （同＋3.4％） 、耐久消費財（同＋3.8％） 、半耐久
消費財（同＋3.1％）の伸び率が拡大し、エネルギー（同▲0.6％）のマイナス幅が縮小した。 
サービスでは、宿泊料（8 月：前年比▲1.4％→9 月：同＋4.6％）の伸び率がプラスに転じた
ほか、外国パック旅行費（8 月：同＋9.7％→9 月：同＋23.5％）のプラス幅が拡大した。これ
ら 2 品目だけでコア指数の前年比を 0.1％pt 程度押し上げた。また、保育所保育料（8 月：同
▲21.6％→9 月：同▲13.3％）のマイナス幅が縮小した。東京都が 25 年 9 月に無償化を拡充し
たことの裏の影響が出た。 
非耐久消費財（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー）では、水道料（8 月：前年比▲3.5％→9 月：同
＋4.9％）の伸び率がプラスに転じた 。東京都の水道料減免が終了したためだ。 食料品では、
冷凍ぎょうざ（8 月：同▲6.5％→9 月：同▲1.3％）などのマイナス幅が縮小した。また、トイ
レットペーパー（8 月：同＋10.5％→9 月：同＋17.8％）など、紙製品の伸び率が拡大した。 
耐久消費財では、冷蔵庫（8 月：前年比▲1.8％→9 月：同＋2.6％） 、パソコン（ノート型）
（8 月：同▲3.2％→9 月：同＋0.8％）などの伸び率がプラスに転じたほか、テレビ（8 月：同
＋10.7％→9 月：同＋14.8％）のプラス幅が拡大した。半導体価格高騰の影響が続いている。 
半耐久消費財では、ゴルフクラブ（8 月：前年比＋11.3％→9 月：同＋43.3％） 、自動車バッ
テリー（8 月：同＋1.4％→9 月：同＋7.0％） 、自動車タイヤ（8 月：同＋0.1％→9 月：同＋
1.4％）などの伸び率が拡大した。 
エネルギーでは、都市ガス代（8 月：前年比▲0.8％→9 月：同＋0.2％）の伸び率がプラスに
転じた。政府による補助金額は増加したが、資源高の影響を相殺するには至らなかった。電気
代やガソリンの前年比は前月から横ばいだった。

4 / 6

先行き：原油高などの波及で、コア CPI は26 年度末にかけて＋3％超を予想 
先行きの物価上昇率について、コア CPI 上昇率は 26 年度末にかけて前年比＋3％超まで高ま
るとみている。当面、原油高の幅広い財・サービスへの波及が物価を押し上げる主因となるだ
ろう。また、円安や、賃上げの価格転嫁も物価上昇につながるとみられる。 
原油価格の動向には注意が必要だ。米国とイスラエルが、米中間選挙後にイランへの攻撃を
再開する計画を策定したと伝えられる2など、中東の和平に向けた先行きは未だ不透明である。
原油価格の代表的な指標であるWTI 先物価格は 10 月 22 日終値で 99 ドル/バレル程度と、中東
情勢の悪化前よりも大幅に高い状況が続いている。さらに、代替調達の進展などを受け、輸入
原油価格は上昇が続くとみられる3。 
こうした原油高は、直接的にエネルギー価格を押し上げる。電気・ガスの燃料費調整制度で
は、3-5 カ月前のエネルギー資源の平均輸入価格が料金に反映されるため、原油高がラグを伴
って波及する。関連して、政府は9 月使用分（10 月請求分）まで電気・ガス料金の補助を実施
しているほか、27 年1-3 月にも同様の措置を検討していると報じられている4。しかし、25 年
夏、26 年冬にも同様の政策を実施しているため、前年比でみた物価上昇率の抑制効果は限定的
なものとなるだろう（巻末図表「エネルギー価格のコアCPI への影響とその見通し」）。また、
原油高は、エネルギー価格だけでなく、輸送費や原材料費の増加を通じて幅広い品目の価格に
も波及するため5、基調的な物価への上昇圧力となるとみられる。 
併せて、ドル円レートは足元で150 円/ドル台後半であり、前年同期比では円安水準にあ
る。原油価格の上昇も相まって輸入物価への押し上げ圧力が強い状態であり、消費者物価への
波及が見込まれる。 
また、賃上げによる人件費の増加分を販売価格に転嫁する動きも継続するだろう。26 年度春
闘における定期昇給（定昇）相当込みの賃上げ率は、加重平均で 5.01％と高水準を維持してい
る6。さらに、26 年度の最低賃金の全国加重平均額も前年度比 5％程度引き上げられる7。こう
した賃金上昇は、サービス価格を中心に物価を押し上げると見込んでいる。 
これらの物価上昇圧力は27 年度にかけても継続するだろう。他方、政府は飲食料品にかか
る消費税率を27 年4 月から2 年間1％に引き下げる方針である。引き下げが実施されれば、27
年度のコアCPI 上昇率は1％pt 超押し下げられ、前年比＋1％台まで縮小するだろう。

23 日）

M
Idea
TSMC  | Asia Pacific
No slowdown of AI semi
demand; reiterate OW
TSMC is further expanding capacity after considering other
constraints, such as data center build, power and other
components supply.  We think the risk of over-capacity is small,
and stay OW.
TSMC sees robust AI semi demand: Despite CSP power shortages and rising
financing costs, TSMC sees demand far above its capacity. It sees no signs of a
slowdown, which may ease some market concerns. The company did not update its

AI semi CAGR guidance (55-60%), saying it is not meaningful as  the demand is
expanding rapidly.
Comments on  wafer foundry partnership (TeraFab) and competition (Intel): For
overseas fabs, TSMC is evaluating a TeraFab partnership, but TSMC must run the
partner fab. TSMC is also considering other fab campus locations, including Texas.
We asked management about IDM outsourcing from Intel and implications for A14
capacity demand. Management said it would try to be fair to all customers. On

competition, TSMC would expand capacity and “leave no food on the table” for
competitors.
On advanced packaging, we asked whether TSMC would help customers using
Intel EMIB-T advanced packaging: The company is confident about 9x and 14x
reticle size chip support. TSMC’s principle is to help customers ramp products
smoothly, so we take it as no objection if Google/MediaTek needs to adopt CoWoS/
CoPoS in future TPU versions, as we articulated in MediaTek: TSMC CoWoS support

eases MediaTek's 2028 TPU production concerns
We raise 2027/28 capex estimates to factor in 2nm and 1.4nm expansion: The
company maintained its 2026 capex guidance (US$60-64bn) and didn’t provide
next-year capex guidance. That said, it  mentioned more than a doubling of 2nm
capacity build by 2027.  Our initial cross checks with equipment venders suggest
EUV tool bookings double in 2027 (vs. 2026), followed by 25-30% growth in 2028.
We therefore raise our 2027/28 capex assumption to US$83/92bn.

3Q26 results review – margin miss, but revenue upside: Pease see Exhibit 1  and
Exhibit 2  for our earnings review. We lower our 2026 and 2027 margin
assumptions,  offset by higher revenue. We maintain our price target at NT$3,088
and reiterate our OW rating.

TSMC 3Q26 earnings and 4Q26 guidance  review
3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance review – margin miss, but revenue upside: 3Q26
margin of 66.4% misses our forecast of 67.5%, but was largely in-line with consensus,
given the margin dilution of 2nm node and overseas operation dilution.
The 4Q26 margin midpoint of 66% shows continued pressure. TSMC guides for 4Q26
revenue to grow 14% Q/Q at the midpoint on very strong demand, in line with our 10-15%

Q/Q expectation. Full-year revenue guidance is raised to 44% Y/Y, also similar to our
preview.
Exhibit 1: TSMC: 3Q26 earnings review

TSMC: Earnings estimate revisions
Our earnings forecasts decline 3% for 2026,  3% for  2027 and is largely unchanged  for
2028: We lower our 2026/27 earnings forecast mostly due to the margin miss of 3Q26
results and weaker than our initial expectation of 4Q26 margin guidance, and also the
wider overseas fab margin dilution into 2027. We slightly raise the revenue assumption for
2027/2028 as TSMC suggested it would speed up the 3nm/2nm capacity build, but that

also comes with larger depreciation and margin pressure when new production ramps.

Our PT is unchanged at NT$3,088. Our PT implies a 20x P/E multiple on our new 2027

EPS forecast. TSMC currently trades at 17x that estimate, close to its average NTM P/E of
16.5x since 2018. We find the stock attractive at current levels.
We continue to derive our 12-month price target (base-case scenario) using a residual
income valuation framework. Our key assumptions are unchanged, including a cost of
equity of 9.2% (beta of 1.2, equity risk premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of 2.0%), an
intermediate growth rate of 10.5%, and a terminal growth rate of 4%.

Our bull and bear case scenario values remain at NT$3,720 and NT$1,700, respectively.

MS Rating
▪ Reﬂecting robust AI capex guidance from

global CSPs, we are OW on TSMC.
▪ Our estimates reﬂect TSMC’s strong
proﬁtability and another year of >40% Y/Y
revenue growth seen for 2026.
▪ Leading-edge (7nm below) demand
remains robust.
▪ Reﬂecting increased bargaining power,

View descriptions of Risk Rewards Themes here
NT$3,720.00
24x 2027e EPS
TSMC dominates foundry services: 1)
Breakthrough in EUV tech and materials
accelerates node migration to 3nm and
beyond. 2) Intel outsources its server CPU
production to TSMC sooner than expected.
3) Intel or Samsung exits leading-edge
foundry business. 4) New tech megatrends
such as 6G or AI drive global semi revenue

growth and increase demand for leading
edge nodes. 5) Cost per transistor decreases
further along with node migration.
NT$3,088.00
20x 2027e EPS
Leading position in leading-edge logic
foundry services with some gross margin
expansion: 1) Global semi revenue CAGR is
sustained in the high single digits in 2025-
28. 2) TSMC continues to lead in market
share in 16nm, 7nm, 5nm, 3nm, and 2nm. 3)
It continues to observe robust AI-related
demand. 4) Cost per transistor does not

decrease meaningfully in 3nm and 2nm,
while capex per k is more demanding than
for 7nm and 5nm.
NT$1,700.00
11x 2027e EPS
TSMC's dominance in foundry business
erodes: 1) Global ex-memory semi revenue
growth is weaker than expected in 2025-28.
2) Declining trailing-edge market share
causes revenue shortfall. 3) Intel's
contribution is weaker than expected. Intel
and/or Samsung successfully develop
leading-edge process technology. 4) Intel's
foundry business execution proves

successful and weighs on TSMC with order
competition. 5) Demand for 2nm is less than
expected given high transistor cost for
foundry customers.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Nien Made Enterprise

3Q preview: EPS +13% YoY to NT$6.6; 
Better 2H but not yet a turnaround

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 441.00 TWD | Price: 358.50 TWD

3Qe EPS NT$6.6: strong rev and GP offset by opex and FX 
Nien Made is scheduled to report 3Q26 results on 4 Nov. Disclosed monthly sales imply 
3Q26 revenue growth of 16% YoY to NT$8.5bn, or 9% in USD terms, largely 
volume-driven. Growth reaccelerated from 1.7% YoY in 1H26, led by custom-made on a 
low US base and expansion in Europe and Japan. Ready-made also improved as 
Cambodia labor shortages eased. (See Exhibit 1 for US window coverings spending 
trends). Gross margin: We expect underlying GPM to rise 1.1ppt YoY to 59.8% on lower 
tariffs, broadly flat QoQ. We forecasts an NT$300mn of IEEPA tariff refunds in 2H26. 
Assuming NT$150mn is booked in 3Q, this would add 1.7ppt and lift reported GPM to 
61.5%. We expect the selling expense ratio to fall 1.7ppt QoQ to 17.5% on lower fuel 
charges, but remain 0.5ppt higher YoY due to continued salesforce and distribution 
network investment. We expect an FX loss in 3Q26 vs. an NT$118mn gain in 3Q25, 
weighing on YoY earnings growth. We forecast 3Q26 EPS of NT$6.6, up 13% YoY. 
US window covering picking up, but no turnaround yet  
BofA credit/debit card data suggests US spending on window coverings turned positive 
in Aug-26 for the first time after 19 consecutive months of YoY declines. Spending rose 
4%/3% YoY in Aug/Sep, respectively. This improvement partly explains the recent 
reacceleration in Nien Made's monthly sales. That said, we view the improvement more 
as normalization off a low base than the start of an industry turnaround. The Aug-Sep 
2025 comparison base was relatively weak, while the current rate-hike environment 
remains a headwind to housing-related demand. 
Slightly raise FY26 EPS est. to NT$22.9; PO to NT$441 
We raise our FY26 EPS estimate by 2% to NT$22.9 to reflect the blended impact of 
stronger-than-expected revenue growth and FX loss. We maintain our FY26 DPS forecast 
of NT$17 per share. We raise our PO by 4% to NT$441, as we roll forward our valuation 
basis from FY26E EPS to FY27E EPS. At the same time, we lower our target P/E from 
19x to 17x, reflecting the MSCI removal and a less supportive rate environment.

Company Sector 
Interior Furnishings

Company Description 
Nien Made, founded in 1974, is one of the world's largest 
window-covering manufacturers. It has roughly 50% 
market share in both ready-made (OEM business) shades 
and blinds, and custom-made (own brand) shutters in the 
US. The US market accounted for over 75% of sales. Key 
clients include Home Depot and Wal-Mart for ready-made 
shades and blinds. Nien Made operates its own retail 
network and e-commerce business for its custom-made 
shutters under the Norman brand name in the US and 
Europe.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on Nien Made, mainly given 1) clear 
custom-made growth profile (share gain in the US and fast-
growing DTC in Japan and Europe), 2) its largest customer 
Home Depot is gaining market share, and 3) margin 
expansion potentials backed by favorable products mix.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Nien Made Enterprise (XNRBF) 
Our PO NT$441 is derived from 17x P/E 2027E EPS. This is around 0.5SD above 
historical the one-year forward P/E, which we think is warranted given the visibility of 
the growth profile.

Downside risks are 1) weaker-than-expected Home Depot's sell-through, 2) slow 
recovery of US housing turnover, and 3) higher tariff increases on China exports to the 
US. 4) Loss of key customers.

iQdatabase ®  is our real-time global research database that is sourced directly from our equity analysts’ earnings models and includes forecasted as well as historical data for income statements, balance sheets, and cash

嘉澤：全球前三大 CPU Socket 供應商，伺服器已成營收主軸
全球前三大中央處理器插座CPU socket供應商。依Bishop統計在電腦暨週邊連
接器廠商中全球排名第四，不分行業別的全球連接器排名全球排名第十八。
主要客戶為AMD與Intel。產品涵蓋 CPU Socket、記憶體插槽、高速連接器及
線材，並延伸至 SOCAMM、液冷快接頭與 NPO Socket。
本研究預估，嘉澤伺服器營收由 2026 年221.14 億元增至 2027 年 328.67 億元，
年增 48.6%。成長來自板量回升、CPU新平台提高 Socket 與高速連接器價值，
以及 AI 新產品放量；PC 相關營收占比則由 2025 年 36.60%，降至 2027 年
16.5%，營運重心持續轉向伺服器。

CPU socket是甚麼
CPU 插槽的設計目的是將 CPU 處理器牢固地固定在主機板上，並在設備移動時
有助於防止潛在的損壞。嘉澤主要客戶為AMD與Intel。
資料來源 :premioinc.com
PGA (插針網格陣列)BGA (球柵陣列)LGA (平面網格陣列)項目
針腳在CPU上，插入主
機板的孔位中。
CPU 底部是微小錫球，直
接高溫焊接在主機板上。
CPU 底部是平貼金屬觸點，
針腳做在主機板插槽上。外觀結構
可以。直接拉起拉桿即
可拔插更換。
不行。屬於永久焊接，需
專業 BGA 焊台設備才能拆
卸。
可以。可自由解鎖扣具、更
換或升級 CPU。更換/升級
1. 針腳在 CPU 上，輕
微彎曲時通常還能手動
扳正修復。
1. 體積最薄、最省空間。
適合行動裝置。
1.風險轉移：安裝失誤時損
壞的是主機板，CPU 價值
較高(倍數差距)
主要優點
2.零插拔力，因此「安
裝操作時」把針腳壓扁
的機率幾乎是零。
2. 傳輸路徑最短，高頻與

散熱表現優異。
2.高密度高上限：相同面積
內可容納更多觸點，能支援
更多通道與更高供電，技術
天花板最高。
效能上限受限：針腳物
理體積大，無法做到高
密度排列，高頻與多通
道（如 DDR5）訊號完
整性不如 LGA。
損壞時無法單獨更換 CPU
永久焊接，壞任何一個晶
片通常需要更換整張主機
板。
主機板針腳極度脆弱：主機
板插槽針腳如毛髮般細小，
清理或安裝時極易壓歪，且
幾乎無法手動修復。
主要缺點
已在主流市場中淘汰行動裝置、掌機、筆電的主流桌上型電腦、伺服器的主流2026年市場主流

代理 AI 讓 CPU 工作量增加：從回覆問題，走向執行任務
原先：用戶提出問題；應用程式向模型發送提示；模型生成答案；應用程式將答
案傳回給用戶。主節點CPU負責調度、I/O和系統管理，而GPU則負責繁重的運
算任務。
現在：代理AI不再只是回答一個問題，而是將目標分解成多個步驟，決定下一步
該做什麼，調用多個模型，查詢資料庫，連接API，運行企業應用程序，檢查權
限，檢索內存，驗證輸出，然後再次循環執行整個過程。
CPU負責：編排：管理分解複雜任務的引擎。代理執行和工具呼叫：觸發 API 和
傳統企業軟體。策略與安全：對每個自主操作進行實際檢查。
隨 AI 從模型推論延伸至 Agent 執行，CPU 的角色擴及任務協調、工具操作及資
料處理。AMD 5 月官方文章已描述 CPU：GPU 由過去約 1：4–8，朝 1：1 移
動；Intel Q2 法說進一步表示，目前以顆數計已接近均衡。兩家原廠的觀察，支
持 CPU 在 AI 基礎建設中重要性提高

資料來源 :AMD官網(智能體人工智慧改變了CPU/GPU的平衡)
5

AI 基礎設施正在從「以 GPU 為中心」的架構，走向「GPU 與 CPU 分工協
作」的分散式架構。未來不是只增加 GPU，而是新增獨立的 Agentic CPU
compute layer
New Agentic CPU Racks：新增的 Agent 執行層負責 Agent Control
Plane 與 Tool Execution管理 agent 的任務拆解、工作流調度、狀態管理與
多 agent 協作執行 API 呼叫、SQL 查詢、文件檢索、資料處理、程式執行與
外部工具調用處理權限控管、稽核紀錄、 安全檢查、重試與人工審批核心功
能是把 LLM 的「決策」轉換成企業系統中的「實際行動」

Meta Muse：個人代理產品化，使 CPU 執行環境的需求更具體
Muse 的投資意義在於：大型消費平台開始提供可持續執行任務的個人代理，使
CPU 沙盒需求從技術架構走向實際服務。
與AMD的關係Meta已部署數百萬顆AMD EPYC，並是Venice、Verano 的領先
客戶。因此，Meta 的運算擴張是 AMD CPU 需求的重要觀察對象。
持續在線的虛擬機（Persistent VMs）： Meta Muse 的底層採用了 Muse
Secure VM 架構。這些 VM 即使在使用者沒有主動點開 App 時，也在後台持續
運作，消耗大量的作業系統配置、記憶體管理與安全驗證資源—這些全部屬於通
用運算，只有 CPU 能做。因此當億萬名用戶的 AI 代理同時在線，CPU 的短缺
會遠比 GPU 來得更強烈。Muse 這樣的個人代理規模不斷擴大，對 CPU 的需求
勢必會增長，預計 CPU 與 GPU 的比例將從 1:1往4:1以上走 (intel電話會議)

資料來源 :trendforce, meta
對運算需求的意義Muse 是甚麼
大型平台投入代理服務產品
化

AI 代理
執行環境需要 CPU、記憶體
及系統資源

Secure VM
工具操作與程式執行增加具備瀏覽器，可跨應用處理工作

Meta muse帶動AI agent趨勢，使CPU類股上漲
 Meta Muse 在 9 月 8 日剛上線時，市場仍在觀望它與傳統的問答機器人（如 ChatGPT）
有何不同。直到 9 月 18 日（上線第 10 天），第三方研調機構 Sensor Tower 釋出數據，
顯示 Muse 已經連續數天登頂美國 App Store 免費榜冠軍，13 天內累計下載突破 250 萬
次。這時市場才驚覺，這是一個「現象級爆款」。
 Muse 可以實際幫用戶串接 Gmail、自動比價，甚至與 Shopify 的 Shop Pay 整合代客結
帳。當週一（9 月 21 日）開盤，市場資金首先推升 Meta 股價大漲 11%。CPU類股全面
大漲10%，嘉澤隔日也開盤即漲停，顯示市場有定位上的連動。(如下圖)
 截至9/30，目前該應用僅在美國與加拿大上線2 2天突破了 500 萬次下載。相比之下，
ChatGPT 達到 500 萬次下載花了 56 天，Grok 花了 103 天，Claude 則耗時 492 天。市

場發現 AI 正從「回答問題」走向「替人類實際操作」的實際應用，引發了 AI Agent的邁
向普及的轉折點。
資料來源 :google財經、yahoo股市

CPU 需求同時受惠於通用伺服器與 AI 代理
代理 AI 提高 CPU 需求，但嘉澤面對的市場不只 AI 機櫃；通用伺服器仍
是重要出貨基礎。
DIGITIMES觀察，Agentic AI促使伺服器架構提高CPU與GPU配置比例，
以承擔多輪推理、排程與狀態管理，因而推升全球伺服器CPU需求，預估
2027年全球出貨量將達4,857.4 萬顆，以通用型仍為主力，達3,527.9萬
顆；AI伺服器CPU因配比提高，2027年出貨量將激增至984.5萬顆，年增
率高達84.1%。價值TAM方面，AMD 對長期市場的預期也明顯提高
資料來源 :AMD法說會，digit times

圖片來源：OIF 的《Co-Packaging Framework》第 10 頁、圖 2(d) 21
NPO的核心定義是：將光引擎從傳統的前面板可插拔位置，移動到更靠近交
換晶片（ASIC） 的地方，但光引擎和ASIC仍然是兩個獨立的封裝體。它們通
常被並排安裝在同一塊高性能基板（如矽中介層或有機基板）上
NPO Socket已進入客戶最後認證階段，且達可量產狀態，量產目標落在2027
年第3季，目前未納入估計，等待未來公司給更多指引。
AI資料中心在Scale-up與Scale-out兩種架構同步拉高頻寬需求，嘉澤在NPO、
既有高速銅傳輸與高功率連接方案的產品組合，將對應不同平台導入時程。

平台換代推升嘉澤營收，毛利率效益約遞延2–4季
 平台規格升級 → Socket針腳數增加 → ASP提高 → 伺服器營收增加 → 毛利金額提高
 平台發布後仍須經過客戶驗證、量產爬坡及 滲透率提升，效益通常不會在發布當季反映。
Eagle與Zen 4自4Q22–1Q23導入後，嘉澤毛利率至3Q23開始回升，並於1Q24重返51%
以上，圖形上約遞延2–4季。下一個平台為4Q26至2027年，預計4Q27毛利率回升。

2H26 營運明顯增長 ，投資建議 Trading Buy 。  
投 資 建 議 Trading Buy ： 雖 目 前 時碩 (4566 TT) 處於 CB 緘 默 期 ，
未 提 供 明 確 展 望 ， 但 受 惠 於 航 太 、 散 熱 產 品 出 貨 增 長 ，3Q26 營
運 已 明 顯 攀 升 。 考 量 2026 、 2027 年 營 運 動 能 向 上 ， 投 資 建 議
Trading Buy ， 目 標 價 92 元 (以 202 7 年 EPS 及 PE  17 倍 評 價 )。  
時 碩 積 極 布 局 高 成 長 潛 力 的 利 基 型 市 場 ， 包 含 液 冷 散 熱 系 統 、
人 形 機 器 人 零 組 件 ： 液冷散熱 方面， 時 碩 致 力 於 提 供 一 站 式 的
全 方 位 熱 管 理 解 決 方 案 ， 其 產 品 線 涵 蓋 快 接 頭 、 分 歧 管 、 冷 卻
液 分 配 單 元 、 微 通 道 板 及 技 術 門 檻 較 高 的 水 冷 板 ， 並 能 提 供 不
鏽 鋼 與 銅 等 多 樣 材 質 。公司 已 成 功 獲 得 美 、 日 系 液 冷 系 統 大 廠
訂單，且於 2H26 放量 出貨 ， 預估 散 熱 產 品 佔 202 6 年 營 收 比 重
有望 接近 10% 。 人 形 機 器 人方 面 ， 時 碩 積 極 拓 展 此 領 域 的 關 鍵
零 組 件 業 務 ， 產 品 涵 蓋 諧 波 減 速 機 關 鍵 零 組 件 、 機 器 手 臂 關 節
精 密 機 械 加 工 零 件 。 時 碩 目 前 已 打 入 美 國 與 中 國 廠 商 的 供 應 鏈
體 系 ； 中 國 無 錫 廠 藉 由 地 緣 優 勢 積 極 切 入 關 節 零 件 製 造 。 泰 國
春 武 里 新 廠也 規 劃 將 產 線 逐 步 延 伸 至 機 器 人 相 關 應 用 領 域 。

獲 利 表 現 平 平 ， 但 受 惠 於 航 太 、 散 熱 應 用 出 貨 明 顯 增 長 帶 動 ，

三 方 確 信 ， 並 透 過 節 能 設 備 導 入 、 能 源 管 理 與 監 測 系 統 、 製 程 優 化 、 太 陽 能
及綠電等多元措施推動減碳與能源效率提升，同時強化水資源與廢棄物管
理 ， 藉 由 回 收 再 利 用 、 廢 水 循 環 及 資 源 化 設 備 ， 落 實 減 量 及 循 環 經 濟 ； 原 物

面，時 碩 重 視 員 工 薪 資 福 利、培 訓 與 職 涯 發 展，推 動 性 別 平 等 與 管 理 階 層 多

透 過 定 期 稽 核 與 評 分 機 制，提 升 供 應 商 在 品 質、交 期 與 服 務 上 的 表 現，並 以

業 公 民 」 自 我 定 位，投 入 社 會 公 益 與 在 地 關 懷，減 少 營 運 對 社 區 的 負 面 影 響，
並 維 持 完 善 的 資 訊 安 全 管 理 以 避 免 資 訊 外 洩。在 治 理 面，時 碩 建 構 包 含 獨 立
董 事 與 女 性 董 事 的 董 事 會 結 構，設 置 薪 酬 與 審 計 等 功 能 性 委 員 會，訂 定 涵 蓋
營 運 、 財 務、 法 遵 、 環 境 與 氣 候 變 遷 、 人 資 與 人 權 等 面 向 的 風 險 管 理 政 策 ，
並 透 過 法 說 會、網 站 與 永 續 報 告 與 利 害 關 係 人溝 通，同 時 積 極 參 與 各 項 ESG
獎 項 評 選 ， 以 強 化 公 司 治 理 與 永 續 形 象 。

6 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15 %

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 ， 亦 將 出 具 降 評 報 告 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

Topics  Loss‑making firms are on the rise in China's growth sectors

In China, the number of companies operating in the red is increasing. And the rise is particularly striking in growth 
sectors. One factor behind the increase is the intense competition among firms, which has led to a situation in which 
only the strongest thrive. Another is that many next‑generation growth companies, despite being viewed as having great 
potential, are losing money.

■ Loss‑making firms are on the rise in growth sectors 
In recent years, the number of companies in China's 
growth sectors that are operating in the red has 
increased markedly. The share of loss-making firms in 
all listed firms (below, "loss‑making‑firm ratio") rose 
sharply from 9.6% in 2019 to 26.9% in 2025. Looking 
at the industry distribution of loss‑making firms, from 
2019 to 2022, the ratio increased mainly in 
non‑manufacturing sectors such as real estate and 
accommodation and eating/drinking services, 
reflecting the property market downturn and strict 
stay‑at‑home measures implemented to curb the spread 
of COVID‑19. Later, from 2022 to 2025, the 
loss‑making-firm ratio surged in 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices; general, production, and 
commercial machinery; electrical machine ry; and 
chemicals. These four sectors alone accounted for 
around 60% of the overall increase. 
They are the principal growth sectors in Chinese 
manufacturing. This is evidenced by the fact that the 
growth rates of value added in all four sectors exceed 
the average for Chinese manufacturing. Many of the 
high‑tech manufacturing industries that the Chi nese 
government has prioritized for development are 
included in these sectors. For example, 5G base 
stations and semiconductors fall under 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices. And robots fall under general, 
production, and com mercial machinery. Meanwhile, 
solar cells and batteries fall under electrical machinery, 
and biopharmaceuticals and organic chemical 
materials fall under chemicals.  
Looking at recent developments in the 
manufacturing of information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices, the global 
communications standard began shifting from 4G 
(LTE) to 5G services in around 2019, and ChatGPT was released in November 2022. These developments 
have provided tailwinds for the growth of the sector. Recently, as it has become clear that ordinary 
consumers and companies can make use of AI services on a daily basis, demand for AI‑related devices and 
semiconductors, as well as d ata centers, has soared. In this environment, many firms are focusing on 
strengthening development, manufacturing, and sales, and the sector's value added has continued to 
increase, rising from 780 billion yuan in 2022 to 910 billion yuan in 2025. Yet over  the same period, the 
number of loss‑making firms increased from 113 to 198, and the loss‑making‑firm ratio jumped from 17.2% 
to 30.1%. 
■ Competition among companies means only the strongest thrive 
One reason loss‑making firms are increasing despite operating in growth sectors is the intense 
competition among companies. The fight for market share has led to overheated investment competition

### Page 3

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  October 2026, No.307

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

2 
and price competition. In information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing, 
the rate of growth of non -current assets from 2022 to 
2025 (+35.4%) far exceeded the growth rate of value 
added (+16.9%). This indicates that supply capacity is 
expanding faster than demand. In addition, the 
consumer price index (CPI) increased by just 0.4% 
over the same period. Service prices rose 2.2%, 
reflecting wage increases, while goods prices fell 0.7% 
due to excess supply in manufacturing. 
Information/communications equipment prices 
decreased by 3.5%, larger than the drop for goods 
overall. 
As the selection of the fittest through competition 
among companies has progressed, profits have become 
concentrated at a small number of highly competitive 
firms. The share of profits earned by the top 1% of 
firms in information/communications equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing rose sharply 
from 24.0% in 2022 to 43.3% in 2025. Leading companies are simultaneously establishing positions as 
both "domestic champions" and "global champions."  
■ Next‑generation growth companies are viewed as having great potential even though they 
are losing money 
However, loss‑making firms cannot simply be lumped together and labeled as losers. Globally, investors 
have shown increasing interest recently in companies with high growth potential even if they are operating 
in the red, and China has a considerable number of such firms. 
In information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices manufacturing, one out of 
every two loss‑making firms is a company attracting 
strong investor interest. The Shanghai Composite 
Index rose 28.5% from 3,089 points at the beginning of 
2023 to 3,969 points at the end of 2025. 20 of the 198 
loss‑making firms in 2025 were not yet listed in 2023, 
and if these are excluded, there were 178 companies 
operating in the red as of 2025. Of these 178 firms, 96 
(53.9%) saw stock‑price increases over the period 
2023-2025 that exceeded the rise in the Shanghai 
Composite Index.  
One characteristic of these 96 companies with strong 
relative stock‑price performance is their active 
investment in R&D and intangible assets. Their 
average R&D intensity (R&D investment / sales) in 
2022 was 9.6%, and their ratio of intangible assets to 
value added stood at 60.5%. Intangible assets include 
computer software, patents, copyrights, and 
relationships with customers and suppliers. In contrast, 
the 82 loss‑making firms with weak relative 
stock‑price performance had an R&D intensity of only 
6.5% and an intangible‑asset‑to‑value‑added ratio of just 38.9%. 
As the above has shown, the increase in loss‑making firms in China's growth sectors is notable, and the 
factors behind it include not only the selection of the fittest as a result of competition among companies but 
also the emergence of next‑generation growth firms that are viewed as having great potential. 
  (Shinichi Seki)

Topics  Climate change heightens the risk of growth stagnation in Asia

Climate change could elevate the risk of economic growth flatlining in the ASEAN zone and India by causing labor 
productivity growth and capital accumulation to stall. To mitigate or avert such a scenario, establishing a business 
environment and institutions that promote "adaptation investment" by both the public and private sectors is an urgent 
task.

■ Heavy‑rain and flood damage is becoming increasingly serious in Asia 
Huge downpours and floods 
resulting in human casualties have 
been a frequent occurrence in Asia in 
the past several years. Still fresh in the 
memory is the debris flow that 
occurred in August this year near the 
border between Nepal and China, 
which as of t he time of writing has 
been recognized as a major disaster 
with thousands dead or missing. And 
in November of last year, Southeast 
Asia and South Asia saw 
record‑breaking torrential rains, 
resulting in numerous casualties, 
particularly in Indonesia and Sri  
Lanka. 
One factor that heightens the risks 
of such floods and heavy rains is 
climate change. As temperatures rise, 
the amount of water vapor in the 
atmosphere increases. Therefore, 
global warming could intensify 
torrential rainfall. Although such risk 
is shared a cross Asia, the way its 
effects are felt differs by region. 
Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and 
India are heavily affected by 
monsoons (seasonal winds), with 
many areas experiencing concentrated 
rainfall during the wet season. In such 
areas, warming can furthe r intensify 
monsoon‑driven heavy rains, 
increasing the risks of floods and 
landslides.  
■ Climate change will delay the escape from the "middle‑income trap" 
Climate change not only results in human casualties by way of natural disasters, but may also undermine 
the long‑term economic growth potential of ASEAN and India through various channels. The Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) [2024] conducted a quantitative an alysis of the extent to which climate change 
will weigh on Asian economies over the long term. Based on the IPCC's "very high GHG emissions" 
scenario (SSP5‑8.5), the analysis assumes a 4.7°C rise in the Earth's average temperature by 2100 relative 
to pre-industrial levels, and estimates the difference in real GDP between that and a hypothetical scenario 
without climate change. The results illustrate the long‑term impact on economic growth through declines 
in labor productivity, stagnation in capital accumulation, and downw ard pressure on consumption due to 
sea‑level rise, floods, heat waves, and other phenomena caused by climate change.  
An examination of the results reveals that in every country/territory, the pushdown effect on real GDP 
relative to the no‑climate‑change scenario expands over time, with 2035, 2050, and 2070 each seeing 
greater impact. The pushdown effect for ASEAN and Ind ia is −15.0% to −8.8% in 2050 and −30.2% to

−18.1% in 2070, larger than the effects for China (2050: −4.8%, 2070: −10.3%) and Central Asian countries 
(2050: −1.7%, 2070: −3.8%). Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and India can be considered regions where the 
risk of economic stagnation due to climate change is relatively large.  
This may make it even more difficult for ASEAN countries and India to escape the "middle‑income trap." 
Although their income levels have risen, they still remain far from high‑income status. Indonesia, Malaysia, 
the Philippines, and Thailand became middle‑income countries in the 1990s or earlier, but have still not 
been able to join the groups of high‑income countries. Furthermore, Vietnam and India, which became 
middle‑income countries in the 2000s or later, have yet to enter a high‑growth trajectory like China once 
did. Climate change, by causing labor productivity growth to stall amid rising temperatures and companies 
to curtail investment due to more frequent disasters, may lead to income growth in the ASEAN zone and 
India being delayed further.  
■ Improving the environment to accelerate "adaptation investment" will be important 
To alleviate the pushdown effect of climate change on growth, expansion of "adaptation investment" by 
the public and private sectors is essential. Specifically, this could include improving working environments 
and promoting measures to lighten workloads a nd reduce headcounts in order to offset declines in labor 
productivity caused by rising temperatures, as well as developing infrastructure and strengthening facilities 
to prevent damage to production equipment from sea‑level rise and floods. However, ASEAN  and India 
cannot be said to have sufficiently 
developed the foundations for public 
and private promotion of such 
adaptation investment.

climate‑change adaptation in the form of 
a "readiness" score with three 
components: economic readiness, 
governance readiness, and social 
readiness. Respectively, these 
components assess 1) whether a 
business environment conducive to 
adaptation investment by private -sector 
companies has been put in place, 2) 
whether the effectiveness of institutions

and policies to support private-sector adaptation investment has been secured, and 3) whether tangible and

Looking at readiness in 2024, ASEAN countries and India generally rank in the middle to lower tiers 
globally, with a large gap between them and developed countries. Notably, the Philippines, India, and 
Thailand fall outside the top 100, indicating weak momentum for adaptation investment. Breaking down 
the scores, problems are especially evident in the economic and governance categories. India and Thailand 
have particularly low eco nomic readiness, ranking 113th and 124th, respectively, and five countries 
(Malaysia not included) fall outside the top 100 for governance readiness. Given this situation, to accelerate 
private‑sector adaptation investment, India and Thailand in particular  need to move swiftly to improve the 
financial environment to make it easier to procure necessary funds domestically and internationally, relax 
regulations to encourage the launch of new businesses, and reform labor markets to enable the smooth 
securing of human resources. In addition, for Asia as a whole, it will also be important to boost confidence 
in the institutional factors that enhance application of investment for adaptation by increasing policy 
stability, ensuring the effectiveness of legal systems, and curbing corruption.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As was universally expected, the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) lifted the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6% at its
September 2026 meeting.
This is quite the turnaround from the 11 August meeting,
where an extended period on hold looked to be the base case.

At that time, the data had broken against the RBA’s more
hawkish view, and its published forecasts did not support
further hikes. But the MPB can now point to the emergence of
some of the upside risks to inflation it warned of in August.
The post-meeting statement was notable for its emphasis
on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices
are now much higher than had been assumed in the August
forecasts” – “much higher” is stronger language than one

normally sees in an RBA statement. The RBA’s liaison indicates
that firms are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-
through was characterised as partial, and additional to
domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We
read this as implying that the RBA is concerned about upside
risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction
costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data

centre boom, although in the media conference, the Governor
clarified that risks there were building rather than materialising.
The broader background highlighted in the Governor’s opening
statement to the media conference, is of demand outstripping
supply. The statement evidenced this by noting the stronger
July CPI and June quarter GDP results than expected.
The accompanying statement was notable for the absence of
language about the labour market being a little tight, despite

this being the inter-meeting message from the Governor
and other staff (see, for example, the Governor’s opening
statement on 18 September) and the language in the post-
meeting media conference. Views clearly differ on this: we
note that the Treasury forecasts underlying the May budget
and the recent Intergenerational Review imply both more
spare capacity in the labour market (a lower NAIRU) and faster
growth in potential from wherever the starting point is (higher

productivity growth and trend increase in participation).
Instead, the post-meeting statement characterised the
labour market as easing “broadly as expected”, despite the
unemployment rate already rising higher than the August SMP
forecast of 4.5% for the December quarter.
RBA hikes in September, November follow-up now expected
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The decision was unanimous, suggesting that any qualms
external MPB members might have had about the RBA’s

downbeat view of supply capacity were overruled by resurgent
oil prices and the stronger than forecast CPI and GDP data.
With upside risks being seen to have emerged, the post-meeting
statement language included that the Board “will continue to
do what it considers necessary to bring inflation sustainably
back to target, including increasing the cash rate target further if
needed”, a small but meaningful change from the “if upside risks
materialise” language from the previous meeting, and back to

the tone of the language at the June meeting.
The bar for a follow-up hike in November is low. Indeed,
judging by the post-meeting rhetoric, a November hike is now
the base case, absent a lasting resolution of the Middle East
conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly
lowers the outlook for energy-related costs in Australia. The
bar for hikes beyond that is much higher, given the cumulative
rise in interest rates, and noting that the labour market is

easing and the housing market will likely weaken further.
The August inflation data released the day after the meeting
was in line with our expectations. We therefore continue to
expect Q3 trimmed mean inflation around 0.9%qtr, too high for
the RBA’s comfort, and upside risks for Q4. Likewise, there are
no implications for our rates view from this week’s inflation data.
Forecast changes
• We have updated our financial forecasts to reflect the change

in our rate view.
• Our labour market forecasts are unchanged on the first
decimal place, but there is some modest upside risk to our
unemployment forecast.
• Our updated GDP profile can be found on page 9.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
led the correction to date, the fact that the other capitals
are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest
rates collide with the tax policy changes announced in May’s

budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced
by the continued pull-back in investor credit growth . Westpac
anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For
more, see our latest Housing Pulse.
Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength
in household demand and higher energy prices. Headline PCE
inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased

0.2%mth and 3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the
share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin
Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%, suggesting some easing in
broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household
spending remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent
to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal income
growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling

households opted to draw down savings.
The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the
headline suggested. While the index edged down 0.1pt to
54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive
months. There were some signs of capacity constraints
emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs
up 4.6pts to 56.4, suggesting that firms struggled to keep
pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs

increased. Firms responded by lifting employment, 1.5pts to
52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed
acceleration in cost pressures, with the prices paid index
jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and
ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every major industry
reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the

clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in this week’s busy
Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes,
citing strong inflation, diminished supply capacity and policy
that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing
to progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time
to assess upcoming data. The one thing both agree on is that
inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and
extent of further rate hikes. Our view remains that one further

hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary
Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the cash rate by 25bps
to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given
Governor Bullock had already communicated that upside risks
to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established
by the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.
Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks.

The Middle East conflict was emphasised as the risk that had
“materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude
oil and refined product prices over recent months. The RBA’s
liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of costs
and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic
capacity pressures and the data centre/AI investment boom were
also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.
In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis

explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in November is
low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided
there is no sudden resolution of the Middle East conflict
beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the
outlook for energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying
(trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr), which was
slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s
forecast. As usual, the monthly detail was mixed, but the key

drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-
related components, particularly rents and new dwelling
purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with the
unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel
prices. We have maintained our forecast for a 0.9% lift in
trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building
for Q4, a follow-up rate hike in November appears most likely.
Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending

surprised with a flat reading in August. Services spend fell back
following recent strength that was driven by major sporting
events, but this was offset by an increase in goods spend,
particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending
pulse is shading a touch softer. However, another solid
outcome for business investment could once again be on the
cards for Q3. August’s goods trade data showcased another

significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our
modelling suggests that the local economic impact of such
investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s
latest update revealed that dwelling prices fell another 1.1%
nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics

Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted
support measures aimed at stabilising growth and supporting
the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its
Pledged Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and
expanded quotas for targeted lending programs. Authorities

also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The
subsidy applies to homes with a floor area of 120m² or less
and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of
mortgage costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of
five years. The measures complement reforms announced in
August to improve developer financing conditions and support
housing completion. However, weak credit demand and
confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy

transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement
in activity, although the details were less convincing.
Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts
to 50.1, supported by stronger production and new orders.
Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in
prices, with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and
producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market conditions
remained weak, with the employment index falling a further

0.6pts and remaining below 50 for almost four years. Conditions
in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the
services PMI lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger
construction activity. Input and output prices moved higher,
selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved
to 48.5 and employment increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it
remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some

traction, but weak labour demand, subdued credit growth and
soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another
constructive signal on the economy and reinforced the case
for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved
by 2pts for large manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while
profitability indicators strengthened and capital expenditure
intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect

fixed investment to increase by 11.6% in FY2026, while large
non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%,
pointing to continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both
manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with measures
of excess production capacity becoming less negative
and indicating that demand remains sufficiently strong to
keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also

intensified, with employment conditions becoming more
negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a
progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations
remained anchored around 2%, providing further evidence
that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation
environment. Overall, the survey was consistent with an
economy operating close to capacity and supports the view
that a December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Resilient activity and simmering inflation
While bumpy and uneven, New Zealand’s economic
recovery has continued over the past few months, with
activity showing resilience in the face of continued global
headwinds. Under the surface, however, inflation pressures

are bubbling away, with much of that pressure centred on
parts of the economy that aren’t sensitive to interest rates.
Economic activity getting back on firmer footing
Looking first at recent activity data, the economy has been
finding its feet again after taking a knock in the wake of the
Middle East war. That has been seen most clearly in the jobs
market, with the number of filled jobs rising by 0.9% in the
year to August. While not shooting the lights out, that is still a

noticeable improvement from the early part of the year, when
filled job numbers were falling.
Under the surface, it’s still a very mixed picture. Agriculture
remains the main engine of growth, with the strength in
commodity export earnings helping to boost incomes,
confidence and spending in regions with strong rural backbones.
That’s supported firm job growth in regions like Canterbury
(where filled job numbers are up 1.9% over the past year), Otago

(+1.5%) and Southland (+3.1%). In contrast, jobs growth remains
much more moderate in Auckland (up 0.6%) and Wellington
(up just 0.4%). That town-and-country split is likely to remain a
feature of the economic landscape for some time yet.
Looking ahead, businesses expect trading conditions to
remain resilient through the final months of the year. That
was reflected in September’s Business Outlook survey.
While the survey’s gauges of expected business activity and

general business confidence eased over the past month, they
remain at firm levels, consistent with continued moderate
growth through the latter part of the year. That resilience has
also been evident in the household sector, with consumer
confidence largely steady through September.
The resilience in household and business sentiment is notable,
as fuel prices have taken a big step higher over the past month.
The average price of 91 unleaded has risen by around 49c/litre –

albeit late in the month and likely not fully reflected in surveyed
confidence – taking it back to the levels seen during the early
stages of the Middle East conflict. Those higher fuel prices
have taken a bite out of household disposable incomes, with
spending on fuel up about 22% compared with this time last
year, even though the volume of fuel sold is down around 11%.
But even with the lift in fuel prices, we haven’t seen the same
degree of pessimism as earlier in the year, when fuel prices first

spiked. Notably, those higher fuel prices haven’t spilled over
into a broader lift in output price pressures either (at least at
this stage). Inflation expectations were steady in September’s
household and business surveys. Similarly, the number of firms
planning to raise prices fell back despite a lift in wage and cost
expectations. However, while that stability in pricing gauges is
welcome, measures of inflation pressure remain elevated.
Taken together, recent data reinforce indications that the

economy is shaking off the impacts of the Iran war.
Persistent inflation drivers and the RBNZ
Turning to inflation, the spike in oil prices earlier this year saw
annual inflation climb to 4.1% in the year to June. With local
fuel costs rising again in recent weeks, we continue to expect
inflation to remain above 3% until at least mid-2027.
However, even before the recent rise in fuel costs, inflation had
been running hot for an extended period. Excluding the rise

in food and fuel prices, inflation in the year to June was 2.9%,
and it’s been running close to or above 3% for five years now.
As we discuss in our recent report, much of the strength in
inflation over the past year has been related to administered
costs, such as local council rates, or to sectors that are less
exposed to competitive pressures, such as electricity and
insurance. Those increases have persisted for an extended period,
and we expect further sizeable increases in many of these costs.

This ongoing firmness is a key reason why we expect domestic
(non-tradables) inflation to be higher than the RBNZ expects
over the longer term. It’s also part of the reason why we
expect the RBNZ will ultimately need to raise the OCR by
more than it assumed in its September policy statement.
We are forecasting a 25bp rate rise at the RBNZ’s December
meeting and expect the OCR to peak at 4% next year.
This is a tough environment for the RBNZ. With ongoing

increases in administered prices boosting core inflation at a
time when energy prices are driving headline inflation well
above the target range, it’s hard for the RBNZ to look through
them. The RBNZ had little headroom to absorb upside inflation
surprises, even before the recent spike in oil prices.
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
But getting inflation back to target in the face of an oil price
spike and high administered prices will not be easy and involves
some tough trade-offs. Administered prices tend to be less
responsive to changes in economic conditions. As a result,
interest rate hikes are likely to have a bigger impact on parts of

the economy that are not the main drivers of inflation. Tighter
policy will also weigh on the labour market and near-term
growth, while increasing households’ debt-servicing costs.
This is an uncomfortable but necessary trade-off. Looking
through, or not responding to, these sorts of large and persistent
cost increases would still leave households facing significant
increases in overall living costs. In the long run, it would be
more damaging for households and the economy if generalised

inflation were to become embedded in price setting behaviour.
The Kiwi has lost its wings
Adding to near-term inflation risks has been the drop in the NZ
dollar. On a trade-weighted basis, the Kiwi has fallen around
4% since the start of September, and it’s begun the December
quarter around 4% below the level the RBNZ assumed in its
September policy statement.
The slide in the Kiwi is mainly due to the strength of the US
dollar, with NZD/USD down nearly 5% since the start of

September to sit just below 0.56 at the time of writing. Moves
against other currencies have been more modest. For example,
NZD/AUD is down 2% over the same period.
Technically, NZD/USD seems oversold. In the short term,
trends in the US dollar will dominate, including any response
to the US labour market report due on Friday evening. Any
turnaround in the USD would likely see NZD/USD trade back
towards the top of the 0.5580–0.5680 range that is governing

the pair for the time being.
NZD/AUD will continue to be driven by changing views on
interest rate differentials between the RBNZ and RBA. This
week, NZD/AUD corrected higher from around 0.8070 to as high
as 0.8110 as RBA cash rate expectations were repriced following
the RBA Board meeting and Australian CPI. In the short term, the
bias for NZD/AUD could be lower, given our view that the RBNZ
will pause at its late-October meeting and that the market is

under-pricing the risk of an RBA rate hike in November.
Next week’s QSBO, due on Tuesday, will be the key event
risk, with the inflation and growth gauges closely watched for
signs that might prompt the RBNZ to pause or hike. Markets
had previously priced in a high chance of a hike at the RBNZ’s
October meeting, but in recent days appear to be reassessing
that view.  Pricing for a 25bp hike in New Zealand could be
pared back towards a 50:50 call. The 0.8030–0.8150 range

is likely to contain the pair in the immediate period ahead.
Our medium-term view is for NZD/AUD to rise as the RBNZ
increases the OCR to 4% by the second half of 2027. We’ll
update our views on the NZD and other currencies in our next
Market Outlook, out Wednesday 14 October.
Fiscal update
The big uncertainty on the domestic horizon is, of course,
November’s general election, and at this stage the polling
looks tight. Neither the established left nor right bloc has a

clear majority.
Ahead of the election, the Treasury has released its Pre-election
Economic and Fiscal Update (PREFU), updating the Budget
2026 (BEFU) fiscal forecasts to reflect the latest economic and
fiscal information and a revised economic outlook. This allows
the public to assess the state of the Government’s finances. It
also provides other parties with a baseline against which to
calibrate their spending and taxation policies.
The PREFU revealed a stronger starting point for the

Government’s finances than had been expected in the Budget.
• A deficit of $8.6bn is estimated for 2025/26, down from the
Budget estimate of $11.9bn.
• Similarly, the forecast deficit for 2026/27 has been revised
down significantly to $6.8bn from $11.4bn.
• Thereafter, the OBEGALx deficit is expected to continue
declining gradually, moving to a surplus of $4bn in 2028/29.
This broadly follows the Budget profile, but is stronger
across the forecast period.

• Net core Crown debt is forecast to peak at 43.9% of GDP in
2027/28, below the 46.1% peak forecast in the BEFU. It’s then
forecast to decline to 39.5% of GDP by the end of the forecast
period in 2030/31, in line with the Government’s goal of
reducing debt below 40% of GDP over the medium term.
Given the improved outlook, NZDM announced a $4bn
reduction in the 2026/27 bond programme to $30bn and a
$15bn reduction in the forecast programme across the whole

period through to 2029/30.
The medium-term forecasts in the PREFU should be treated
with caution. The economic forecasts underpinning the
PREFU were finalised on 21 August, when both oil prices
and, especially, bond yields were materially lower than they
are today. Had the forecasts been finalised more recently, the
fiscal projections presented today would have been weaker,
at least in the near term. Indeed, the Treasury is upfront in
acknowledging that, while the risks are two-sided, the balance

is skewed to the downside.
Aside from the obvious uncertainty surrounding the economic
outlook because of unpredictable developments in the Middle
East, the approaching General Election, and the possibility of
a change in the composition of the Government, means the
assumptions underpinning the PREFU forecasts could quickly
become invalidated.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Oct 6, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment declined 5.2% to 84.4 in September,
taking the index back towards the deeply pessimistic levels seen
earlier in the year. Rising fuel prices and fears of an imminent

interest rate rise drove the move with a continued weakening in
housing markets likely adding to unease in some quarters.
Developments over the last month would have added to these
concerns with: the RBA raising the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6%
during the survey week; average pump prices pushing above
$2.36/litre nationally and no end in sight to global supply
disruptions;  and more weak updates on the housing market.
NZ: Q2 NZIER Survey of Business Opinion (index)

Oct 6, General business confidence last: 12.3
Business conditions picked up modestly in the June quarter as
concerns about the war in the Middle East eased. However,
cost and pricing gauges remained firm. The September quarter
survey will be closely watched ahead of the RBNZ’s 28
October meeting. Recent weeks have seen signs that activity
is firming. However, fuel prices have also risen sharply, and we
(along with the RBNZ) will be watching for signs of a broader

lift in inflation pressures. The timing of survey responses
within the sample period will also warrant scrutiny, given that
oil prices rose late in the quarter.

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
11:10 October 09 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
The only data release of note in Australia this week was the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment
Survey, which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At 80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to
deeply pessimistic levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock when readings were among

the weakest in the survey’s more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest rate pressures are
once again clearly front of mind for consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a year ago’

### Page 2

and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0% and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the
RBA’s September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact – sentiment tanked 20% between
those surveyed before the RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.

Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient, but they still weakened, with the one-year
and five-year ahead sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers are becoming more
‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively low
versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip
demand. We expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force Survey – see our preview
for more detail. All told, with higher interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening jobs
market all weighing at once, households are entering the final months of the year on the back foot.

Offshore, the focus was on US data.

Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that September non-farm payrolls came in
softer than expected across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k expected), and there
were sizeable downward revisions to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July and August
payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure &
hospitality and education & health services industries, although construction and manufacturing
employment continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related investment. The unemployment rate
edged up to 4.2%, with softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force participation to
61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average pace of job creation slowing to around 50k.
However, softer payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market deterioration. Lower
immigration and ageing demographics have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more consistent with a labour market that is
gradually normalising than one entering a meaningful downturn.

The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish message, with participants noting they
had "not seen sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and expressing concern that
inflation risks remained "skewed to the upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing persistent goods price strength to the
effects of the AI buildout despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was also concern
that, after more than five years of inflation above target, elevated inflation could begin to influence
inflation expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In contrast, labour market conditions
were generally viewed as stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority noted the
labour market had strengthened somewhat recently, several participants highlighted unusually low
dynamism, characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding rate and elevated long-term
unemployment. Importantly, participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth was moderate
and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2 percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor
market was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".

### Page 3

Related articles
A key focus was the interaction between AI investment, productive capacity and inflation. While
participants broadly expected AI-related investment to boost productivity growth and potential output
over time, several warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand to outpace aggregate
supply over the medium term, putting upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some
participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate and several judged the current policy
stance to be "not restrictive or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants supported
the September rate increase, and most assessed that another increase in the federal funds rate would
likely be appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain data dependent.

The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted a similar picture of an economy that
remains resilient but is gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9 from 55.4 in
August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export
orders, which slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight months. Labour market
conditions improved modestly with the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory 50.1 after
two months below 50, supported by elevated backlogs and still-solid demand. Respondent
commentary illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some firms reporting they were "filling
positions vacated due to promotions or retirements", while others cited "restructuring due to efficiencies
gained using AI tools". Cost pressures also remained elevated, with the prices paid index rising to 74.0,
its highest level since July 2022 and around 18pts above its historical average, while fuel costs, tariffs
and supply chain constraints remained the most frequently cited concerns. Overall, the report suggests
services activity remains consistent with ongoing expansion, albeit at a slower pace, while labour
demand and pricing pressures remain firm.

Overall, the data supports our view that the FOMC will move to a broadly neutral policy stance by the
end of the year with one more rate hike, although the case for a back-to-back move in October has
become less clear cut.

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh được 
thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng 
hơn; trong khi triển vọng nhóm 
khách hàng công nghiệp bị ảnh 
hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan

Đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyện và xử 
lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho phép 
cải thiện doanh thu và lợi nhuận của 
mảng này

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 
785 tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An vận hành từ Q3/2025 
giúp sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế 
quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống 
của Việt Nam.

UBND TP.HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng 
mạnh từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh 
trong thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự 
án của doanh nghiệp ở mảng này sẽ vận hành hiệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và 
lợi nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với 
BWE, giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với 
giá đóng cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh 
 Biwase là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực cung cấp nước sạch với 
thị phần đạt 100% tại Bình Dương. 
Ngoài ra, công ty còn mở rộng sang 
lĩnh vực đầu tư nhà máy xử lý nước và 
chất thải rắn nhằm hoàn thiện hệ 
sinh thái dịch vụ hạ tầng môi trường 
tại khu vực. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Nắng nóng thúc đẩy tiêu thụ nước sạch khối dân cư, đem về tổng doanh thu cấp nước tại Bình Dương và 
Bình Phước năm 2026/2027 đạt 2,953/3,192 tỷ VND (6.8%/7.4%). 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Nhà máy nước Nhị Thành giai đoạn 3 đi vào hoạt động giúp tổng doanh thu nhà máy đạt lần lượt 213/247 tỷ 
VND (+16%/16% YoY) trong 2026/2027 và duy trì tăng trưởng trung bình 15% đến 2030. 
 Tham khảo trong báo cáo kỳ trước:

### Page 4

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh 
được thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng 
nóng hơn; trong khi triển vọng 
nhóm khách hàng công nghiệp bị 
ảnh hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan 
 Dự báo đồng thuận xác suất xảy ra El Nino ở mức xấp xỉ 100% từ 2H2026 kéo 
dài đến tháng đầu 2027 cho thấy cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt 
nắng nóng sẽ gay gắt và kéo dài hơn so với các năm trước. Đây là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY 
(+2.6/1.1 ppts). Trái lại, sau khi section 301 được công bố và có hiệu lực thay 
thế section 122, tình hình thuế quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho 
các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống của Việt Nam. Cụ thể, Section 301 không tự 
hết hạn và có nền tảng pháp lý chắc chắn (ít nhất là trong nhiệm kỳ của 
Trump); đồng thời áp dụng hạn ngạch thuế quan cho một lượng hàng dệt may 
nhất định của một số nước không có Việt Nam, khiến bối cảnh bất lợi cho các 
ngành sản xuất truyền thống. Tuy nhiên, với việc Bình Dương đang tập trung 
đầu tư hạ tầng khu công nghiệp hiện đại nhằm thu hút dòng vốn FDI vào các 
lĩnh vực công nghệ cao, sản lượng nước sạch sẽ được duy trì khi tiêu thụ khối 
sản xuất truyền thống được bù đắp bởi nhóm công nghệ cao. Theo đó, trong 
năm 2026, KBSV điều chỉnh tăng nhẹ dự báo sản lượng nước sạch tại Bình 
Dương & Bình Phước đạt 224.9 triệu m3 (+6.8% YoY), với mức tăng của nhóm 
sản xuất/sinh hoạt/hành chính lần lượt là 5.8%/7.5%/11% YoY.

Biểu đồ 4. Xác suất tăng dần của EL Nino

Nguồn: Bloomberg, KBSV tổng hợp

Mảng cấp nước: Tiến độ điều chỉnh 
giá bán nước gián đoạn do môi 
trường lạm phát leo cao 
 Do tác động của cuộc chiến Mỹ - Iran gây ra gián đoạn dòng chảy vận tải dầu 
tại eo biển Hormuz, giá nhiên liệu trong nước đã tăng mạnh từ đầu năm (Dầu 
Diesel - +64.3%, E5 RON 92 – +40%) và khiến cho tình hình lạm phát trong nước 
diễn biến tiêu cực: CPI bình quân 8 tháng năm 2026 tăng 4.5% với chỉ số giá 
nhóm nhà ở, điện nước, chất đốt và vật liệu xây dựng tăng cao nhất với 6.7% và 
giao thông tăng 5.4%. Trong bối cảnh đó, chúng tôi cho rằng tiến độ điều 
chỉnh giá bán nước tại các thị trường của BWE có khả năng bị đẩy lùi sang 
2027-2028 hoặc cho đến khi tình hình giá nhiên liệu ổn định trở lại. Trong dài 
hạn, khả năng đàm phán giá nước thành công là khá cao bởi: i) Thông tư 
145/2025/TT-BTC có hiệu lực từ 2026 là căn cứ để UBND quy định tỷ suất lợi 
nhuận định mức các dự án nhà máy nước; ii) BWE và cổ đông lớn là TDM dự 
kiến đầu tư và mở rộng công suất cung cấp – phân phối nước tại các vùng hẻo 
lánh nên có khả năng tăng áp lực đàm phán tăng giá nước tại Bình Dương. 
Trong kịch bản thận trọng, KBSV điều chỉnh tăng giá nước sạch trung bình khu

### Page 5

vực Bình Dương 1%/năm từ 2028 – 2030.

Đơn giá xử thu gom, vận chuyện và 
xử lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho 
phép cải thiện doanh thu và lợi 
nhuận của mảng này 
 UBND TP.HCM (bao gồm TP.HCM cũ, Bình Dương, Bà Rịa Vũng Tàu) quy định 
giá cụ thể đối với dịch vụ vận chuyển, xử lý chất thải rắn sinh hoạt trên địa bàn. 
Theo đó, quy định mới thay thế văn bản có hiệu lực từ 2016 sẽ chuyển hình 
thức tính giá đầy đủ theo 3 cấu phần: Thu gom, vận chuyển và Xử lý; với khung 
giá được xác định cho các phường đô thị và xã/nông thôn. So với quy định 
2016, quy định mới điều chỉnh tăng mạnh khung giá khoán đối với chủ nguồn 
thải nhỏ (Hộ gia đình tăng 80-184% đối với khu vực đô thị, 55-140% đối với khu 
vực nông thôn; Hộ kinh doanh nhỏ lẻ tăng 42-150% đối với khu vực đô thị, 20-
138% đối với khu vực nông thôn); đồng thời yêu cầu chủ thải tại nguồn và chủ 
nguồn thải lớn áp dụng khung giá thành (bao gồm giá thu gom, vận chuyển và 
xử lý) nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn chi phí liên quan tới các khâu xử 
lý rác thải thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng việc ban hành khung giá mới tính toán 
đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh phản ánh định hướng phát triển 
hạ tầng xử lý rác thải hiện đại của TP.HCM, đặt trọng tâm vào các nhà máy đốt 
rác phát điện thân thiện và an toàn với môi trường. Đối với doanh nghiệp hoạt 
động trong lĩnh vực môi trường nhiều kinh nghiệm như BWE, cơ chế thu gom, 
vận chuyển và xử lý minh bạch sẽ giúp thúc đẩy hiệu quả các dự án mà doanh 
nghiệp có kế hoạch triển khai trong tương lai. KBSV dự báo doanh thu và lợi 
nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Nguồn: UBND TP .HCM, KBSV

Biwase thành công tăng vốn cổ 
phần lên 2,513 tỷ VND 
 Sau đợt chào báo cho cổ đông hiện hữu với giá chào bán 37,000 đồng/cổ 
phiếu, doanh nghiệp dự kiến huy động được khoảng 1,162.5 tỷ VND, nâng vốn 
điều lệ lên mức 2,513 tỷ VND. Khoản vốn bổ sung được dự kiến sử dụng cho ba 
mục đích chính: thanh toán nợ vay đến hạn (khoảng 773.3 tỷ đồng), đầu tư dự 
án nhà máy điện rác công suất 24MW (225 tỷ đồng) và mua cổ phần tại CTCP 
Cấp nước Ninh Thuận (hơn 164 tỷ đồng), qua đó biến doanh nghiệp này trở 
thành công ty liên kết. Dự kiến, lợi nhuận đóng góp từ công ty liên kết trong 
2026/2027 tiếp tục tăng trưởng tích cực, đạt 238.1/245.2 tỷ VND (40%/3% YoY).
